```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
```

```
import time
import numpy as np

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    # Ensure we exit early if budget is already nonpositive.
    # Determine a safe length from parent construction if available.
    n = 1
    try:
        parent = height_sequence_1
        # If parent is not finite? We only need size; but if it is weird, np.asarray may cost? It's an array.
        arr = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size >= 1:
            # cap to avoid accidental external evaluation blow-up; 6733 known, or maybe 4096.
            n = int(arr.size)
    except Exception:
        n = 1
    if n < 1:
        n = 1
    # We know length 6733 is safe; if parent unexpectedly huge, use 6733 or 1024.
    if n > 20000:
        n = 6733 if n >= 6733 else 20000
    # Optional: If time budget is extremely small, no internal eval.
    seq = [1.0] * n
    try:
        # verify if the exact evaluator is available and n small enough; for 6733 okay.
        if n <= 20000: # but evaluating 20000 O(400M) maybe too slow. Use threshold 10000? Maybe just if n <= 8192.
            score = evaluate_sequence(seq)
            if not np.isfinite(score):
                seq = [1.0]
                score = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return seq
```

Let's focus on numerical optimization. We can use n=6733 and perform iterations using FFT. Need practical algorithm to reach 1.502. Let's design search.

Objective F(a)=2n max c / S^2. Scale invariant. We can normalize S=1 at each step. The current M = max c. We want reduce M. A natural algorithm: gradient of smooth max or "leveling" where we subtract from a a vector raised by high c coefficients, then renormalize. Since if c_k high, gradient for coordinate i is sum_{j: i+j=k} a_j (weighted). We can compute for a set of peak lags H: d_i = ∑_{k∈H} a_{k-i}. Then update a_i ← a_i - λ d_i (or multiplicative) to reduce pairs contributing to peaks. Then clip to nonnegative and renormalize, prefer mass on coordinates with low gradient. This is like power method. We can iterate.

Another algorithm from SOTA: For fixed a, solve LP to maximize sum b subject to b≥0, a*b ≤ M. The solution b_i likely positive where gradient low. Then update a toward b. This is like finding a feasible direction that increases sum while keeping M, then rescale lowers M. We can approximate LP by gradient/projection or use CVXPY for small n? But n=6733, LP with 13465 constraints, 6733 vars; maybe CBC too slow. Matrix-free subgradient easier.

We can also formulate as iterative "water-filling": Given current a and target U, find b≥0 sum=1 with a*b ≤ U. If exists, replace? The LP maximize sum b under a*b≤U. If current U = M(a), a itself feasible sum=1. Maximizing sum b can produce b with sum S_b≥1. Rescale to sum=1; since a*b≤M, and sum=1, its max convolution with itself? Not necessarily ≤M? But the update f ← (1-t)f + t g. If g = S_b b with sum sqrt(2n)? Hmm.

Maybe direct multiplicative gradient will suffice. Let's test conceptually. Start with a continuous one-sided power (α near 0.5) score ~1.57. Apply iterations to flatten residual peaks (probably center). The center coefficient c_center = ∑ a_i a_{n-1-i}. To reduce it, gradient d_i = a_{n-1-i}. Multiplicative update a_i ← a_i exp(-η a_{n-1-i}) reduces products with high reverse. This will redistribute mass to make a and reverse anti-correlated. The resulting sequence may develop oscillations/zeros. After renormalization, score may drop. Need avoid clipping to zero too much causing new peaks.

Potential issue: The objective max includes all peaks; if we level highest coefficients by dynamic weights w_k (softmax), d = convolve(a, w_padded). Multiplicative update `a *= exp(-η d / max a?)` or `a = exp(log(a+ε) - η d)` then renormalize. This is gradient descent on φ(c,ε) with log-sum-exp. For large n, FFT.

Let's derive gradient of logsumexp(c, ε): w_k = exp(ε c_k /?)/Z. d_i = ∑_k w_k a_{k-i} = (w * a)_i (valid range). Update with nonnegative projection: a_i ← a_i * exp(-η d_i / scale). Renormalize to sum1. Since c scale with a? If normalize sum1, c O(1/n). gradient d O(1/n). Need step size.

Alternatively, use power iteration: Let u = d (gradient). New a proportional to a / (max(u,?)) or a*(1 - u). In "minimum collision" algorithms, one uses `a_i <- a_i * (1 - gamma * u_i)` and projects.

Can we guarantee monotonic? Not needed. But need robust best tracking.

Implement convolution via FFT for surrogate:
```
def conv_full_exp(a, b=None):
    if b is None: b=a
    L = 1 << (len(a)+len(b)-1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    B = A if b is a else np.fft.rfft(b, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*B, L)[:len(a)+len(b)-1]
    return c
```
For a real nonnegative, c should be nonnegative but FFT small negatives; clip.
Need gradient d_i =∑_k w_k a_{k-i}. We can compute using FFT as described:
```
def grad_from_weights(a, w):
    n=len(a)
    wr = w[::-1]
    L = 1 << (n + len(wr) -1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); W=np.fft.rfft(wr,L)
    f=np.fft.irfft(A*W,L)[:n+len(wr)-1]
    d = f[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]  # indices n-1...2n-2
    return d
```

Algorithm for each init:
```
a = normalize(init)
best_a = a; best_score = exact(evaluate?) maybe surrogate
for it in range(max_iter):
   c = fftconvolve(a,a); c=np.abs? clip
   M = c.max()
   surrogate_score = 2*n*M  (sum normalized1)
   if surrogate_score < best_surr - tol: update best if time.
   # weights
   c_pos=np.maximum(c,1e-300)
   # log weights for p
   if p>1:
      w=np.exp((p-1)*(np.log(c_pos)-np.log(M))); w/=w.sum()
   else w=c/M
   d=grad(a,w)
   mu=np.dot(a,d)
   sd=np.sqrt(np.dot(a,(d-mu)**2))+1e-300
   step=eta # maybe 0.5
   loga += -step*(d-mu)/sd + noise
   loga -= loga.max()
   a=np.exp(loga)
   # clip very small? a[a<tiny]=tiny? But zeros allowed; keep positive.
   a /= a.sum()
```

Search procedure:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42,budget_s=1300):
 start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s-5
 rng=default_rng(seed)
 # choose n as parent size if safe else 2048/4096
 # best = None
 # candidate init list
 for init in inits:
    a=init()
    # maybe exact initial if cheap
    score=evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()) if exact_budget else surrogate
    best update exact
    # run multiplicative iterations for allocated time/iters
    for it in range(max_iter_per_init):
       if time>deadline-20: break
       c=fft_conv_square(a)
       M=c.max()
       # weights
       # p adaptive: start 4, double every 100 if no improvement
       w=softweights(c,p)
       d=grad(a,w)
       # maybe combine with previous d momentum
       loga = np.log(a+eps)
       mu=float(np.dot(a,d))
       var=float(np.dot(a,(d-mu)**2))
       step = step_size * (1 - it/...?) maybe adaptive
       loga -= step*(d-mu)/(sqrt(var)+eps)
       # optional random noise
       if noise>0: loga += rng.normal(0,noise,n)
       loga -= loga.max()
       a=np.exp(loga)
       # Set very small to 0? keep tiny = np.exp(-50) to allow log again. We can add floor: a[a < tiny]=0? If zero, log next issue. Use floor = 1e-300? Exp underflow okay. To keep log finite, before log add eps=1e-300.
       a /= a.sum()
       # surrogate score
       if it%10==0:
          c maybe we have? M current. surrogate=2*n*M
          if surrogate < best_surr - 1e-4: exact eval if time
```
But recomputing c after update? For surrogate, we can compute c at start of next iteration. To update best exact, need a candidate. We can use current M before update as score of current a. At end of iteration after update, no c. We can evaluate exact occasionally on a current (updated) by computing exact; no need surrogate.
Pseudo:
```
best_exact_array, best_exact_score = initial exact
for it in range(max_iter):
   if time... break
   c = fftconv(a)
   M = c.max()
   surr = 2*n*M
   if surr < best_exact_score - 1e-6 and exact_eval_allowed:
       exact = evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())
       if exact < best_exact_score: update
   # update a (if not last)
   weights from c
   a = update
# final exact for best candidate
```

Let's define `compute_update(a, c, eta, gamma_temp, noise)`:
```
M=c.max()
range_c = max(M - np.mean(c), 1e-30)
t = 0.1*range_c + 1e-300
w = np.exp((c - M)/t)
w /= w.sum()
d = grad_conv(a,w)
mu = np.dot(a,d)
dev = d - mu
var = np.dot(a, dev*dev)
if var <= tiny: return a, False
loga = np.log(a + 1e-300)
loga -= eta * dev / np.sqrt(var)
if noise: loga += rng.normal(0,noise,n)
# Keep loga bounded: if loga.min() < -700, add 700-min? But exp after max subtract.
loga -= loga.max()
a_new = np.exp(loga)
# To avoid all zeros from underflow, if sum<=0 fallback
s=a_new.sum()
if not finite or s==0: return a, False
a_new /= s
return a_new, True
```

For given a (sum1), current M. We want find b≥0 sum1 with `a*b ≤ M` (or tight). If such b exists with sum1, and its self-convolution max smaller? The LP maximize sum b under `a*b ≤ M`. We can approximate b by iterative scaling:
- Start b=a.
- For current b, compute r=a*b. Constraints r_k≤M.
- We want increase b where its contribution to violations low. Multiplicative: `b_i *= exp(η(1 - r_contrib_i/M?) )` where r_contrib_i =∑_k a_{k-i} b? That is a*b? Actually to keep r≤M, a simple update: b_i ∝ b_i / (τ a*b? + ε). This is like solving linear complementarity. There is an algorithm for maximum packing: iterate `b_i <- b_i * (1 - ρ * (r_i?))`?
Constraints are on linear combinations of b: (A b)_k ≤ M, b≥0, objective ∑b. This is a linear program. We can solve its dual approximately: find nonnegative y_k such that A^T y ≥ 1, minimize M∑y. Then b supported where A^T y=1. This dual is easy: start y uniform, update y_k ← y_k * max(1-r_k/M, 0)? Sinkhorn-like. Specifically, primal: max 1^T b s.t. A b ≤ M, b≥0. Dual: min M 1^T y s.t. A^T y ≥ 1, y≥0. Complementary slackness: b_i>0 => (A^T y)_i=1. This is like IAM's mutual information? We can iterate:
   Given y, thresholding b_i = 1 if A^T y_i ≤1? For LP optimum extreme. To update y: For constraints k with b_j>0 and a_{k-j}>0? constraint tight if active. Could use gradient: y_k += η max(0,(A b)_k/M -1). And b_i += η max(0,1 - (A^T y)_i). This is a primal-dual method for feasibility. Then rescale b sum1. This might produce a better direction. Consider sequence a_i = [1,0,0,1,0,0,...]? Isolated ones every3: score? n large, m=n/3, paircount max ~? For isolated, c_k counts pairs whose sum k in arithmetic progression. What about a_i = 1 on a *Sierpinski* set with low pair sums? To get maxpair≈m^2/(2n), density p=m/n. Need pair sums uniform. This is a "2-design" subset. There are known balanced incomplete block designs where every sum appears about p^2 n. For cyclic groups, a subset with flat difference/sum count is a "perfect difference set" or "cyclic projective plane". Difference sets control differences, not sums, but a set's sumset flatness is equivalent to its difference set flatness under mapping i→-i? Let's check: The number of ordered pairs (x,y)∈A×A with x+y=k. Let y'=-y mod N. Then x+y=k -> x - y' = k. Thus the sum counts of A equals difference counts of A' = {-A mod N}. So if A is a perfect difference set (or has flat differences), then A's sum counts are flat! Great! In a cyclic group of length N, difference set D: for each nonzero d, #x,y∈D x-y=d = λ. Then for B = -D, #x,y∈B x+y=k for k≠0 also λ; for k=0, #x=y=N? actually |B|. Thus sum counts flat off zero. If we take a binary sequence supported on a difference set in a cyclic group, its *cyclic* convolution is flat except at 0. For linear convolution of a single period, if support is a cyclic difference set and we consider the sequence on length N (not repeated), the linear convolution coefficients for sums modulo? The linear convolution counts ordered pairs with exact sum k in [0,2N-2]; the cyclic convolution modulo N combines c_k + c_{k+N}. If cyclic C_k flat λ for k≠0 and |D| at k=0, linear coefficients may be split but each ≤λ, except maybe edges where k+N? Since c_k and c_{k+N} nonnegative and sum to cyclic C. Potential performance of `_gradient_from_weights`: It uses `np.fft.rfft` for a and w_rev. We can cache A for c and gradient if using same L. Use L=next pow 2*(3n-2)? For square conv length 2n-1 fits. For gradient conv length 3n-2. Use L = 1 << (3*n-1).bit_length()? For n=6733, 3n-1=20198 bit_length15 ->32768. Good.
```
def _fft_square(a, L=None):
   if L is None: L = 1 << (2*len(a)-1).bit_length()
   A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
   return c,A,L
```
For gradient need A of same L; reuse if L same. `A` length L/2+1. W=np.fft.rfft(wr,L). h=irfft(A*W,L)[:3n-2]; d=h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]`.
But square conv with L=32768 is okay; using larger L than minimal maybe slight. Use same L for both.

Accuracy: FFT convolution may have small imaginary/negative; `c = np.real(c)`, `c = np.maximum(c,0)`. The true c symmetric; we can average c and c[::-1]/2 to enforce symmetry and reduce noise? c should be symmetric exactly? For convolution of real sequence with itself, c symmetric: c_k=c_{2n-2-k}. Init generation details:
```
def _init_random_positive(n,rng,seed_idx):
   # mixture: start with 0.2 + rng.exponential? 
   a = rng.exponential(1.0, n)
   a += 0.2
   # maybe apply spatial frequency
   idx=np.arange(n)
   mod=1+0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*rng.random()*idx)
   a*=mod
   a=np.clip(a,0,None)
   if a.sum()<1e-6: a=rng.random(n)+0.1
   return a/a.sum()
```

Can we improve by directly minimizing a penalized sum of peaks rather than logsum? The update with weights top peaks likely reduces max but may ignore multiple peaks. The softmax weights over c handles all.

Let's choose weights `w`:
```
M = c.max()
# Use temperature based on peak excess over median
med = np.median(c)  # O(n logn), c length 13k; okay but each iter cost. Use mean maybe.
scale = max(M - 0.9*np.quantile(c,0.9)? expensive. Use std.
temperature = max(0.05*(M - c.mean()), 1e-30)
w = np.exp((c - M)/temperature)
```
Computing mean O(n) cheap. If c broad, top few. As flatten, M-mean small, temperature small; weights more top. Good.
Could use `temperature = max(0.02*(M - c.min()), 1e-30)` but c.min ~0. Use M mean.

Step adaptation: Keep `eta=0.5`. If surrogate after update (computed at next loop) < previous M, maybe increase slightly; else decrease. We can store `prev_M`. But since we update then recompute, we can do:
```
if it>5:
   if newM < M: eta = min(1.5, eta*1.05); gamma=max(0.5,gamma*1.05)
   else: eta *=0.8; gamma*=1.2
```
But if no update accepted? We always update. Could revert if newM > M*(1+0.2). Implement trial as above:
- After computing c/M, generate a_new.
- Compute c_new and M_new quickly (we need for next loop anyway). If M_new > M * (1 + 0.05) and it>20: revert (keep a, c,M) and adjust eta/gamma (eta*=0.7, gamma*=1.2) and continue without using a_new. This doubles FFT only on failures. But maybe many failures if step too big. Good.
- If accepted, set a=a_new, c=c_new, M=M_new, update best.
This uses one square FFT per accepted iter plus one per rejected. Inside loop, we already have c; for trial we need c_new. We can use c_new for next accepted. Good.

Exact eval: when M_new < best_exact/(2n) - tol etc. Since best_exact score = 2nM_exact. Surrogate M_new corresponds score 2nM_new. We can exact if `2nM_new < best_exact - 1e-6` and time. Or periodically to verify if surrogate best. We'll do:
```
if (score_new < best_exact_score - 1e-5 and exact_count < max_exact and time-last_exact > 1.0) or (it % exact_period == 0 and score_new < best_exact_score + 0.1): exact
```

Implementation details for `_square_fft`:
```
def _square_fft(a, L):
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)
    c = c[:2*n-1]
    c = 0.5*(c + c[::-1])
    c[c<0] = 0
    return c, A
```
A for gradient: If use L same, A available. But if c symmetrization changes no need A. `_grad_weights(a,w,A,L,n)`:
```
wr = w[::-1]
W = np.fft.rfft(wr, L)
h = np.fft.irfft(A*W, L)
d = h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Need A from square FFT of same a. Good. But after update, a changes; recompute A.

If using `scipy.signal.fftconvolve`, less code but maybe not imported? We can import scipy numpy. But manual fine.

```
def _exact(a):
   # optional cap? a finite nonneg
   try:
      s = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))

   except Exception:
```

Pseudo:
```
def _run_search(n, rng, time_limit, global_best, exact_limit):
   L=...
   starts = _make_starts(n,rng)
   best_local = None
   max_iter = int(time_limit * 100)  # estimates
   total_exact = 0
   for idx,a in enumerate(starts):
      if elapsed>time_limit: break
      a = a.copy()
      # normalize
      # exact initial if cheap: if n<=2000 or exact_limit>0
      sc = _exact(a) if allowed else inf
      update global
      eta=0.6; temp_frac=0.08; noise=0.1
      no_improve=0
      for it in range(max_iter // len(starts)?):
         if elapsed>time_limit: break
         c,A=_square_fft(a,L)
         M=c.max()
         score=2*n*M
         # exact if score < global_best - 1e-8 and allowed
         if allowed_exact and (score < global_best[0] - 1e-7 or it%exact_period==0): ...
         # update
         w = weights(c, temp_frac)
         d = grad(a,w,A,L)
         # if variance tiny, break to next init
         mu = dot(a,d); var=dot(a,(d-mu)^2)
         if var < 1e-30: break
         a_new = log update(eta, noise)
         c_new,A_new = _square_fft(a_new,L)
         M_new=c_new.max()
         if M_new < M*(1+1e-12): accept
            # adapt eta up
         else:
            # try half eta
            a_new2 = log update(eta*0.5)
            c_new2... if better accept else eta*=0.7; if repeatedly fail break
         noise *=0.99
         temp_frac *=1.02? maybe focus
      # after init, if global_best < target and time maybe continue
   return global_best
```
This can be long. Need not be perfect.

Weights function:
```
def _weights(c, frac):
   M=c.max()
   scale = max(M - c.mean(), 1e-300)
   t = max(frac*scale, 1e-300)
   w = np.exp((c-M)/t)
   s=w.sum()
   if not finite or s==0: w=np.zeros_like(c); idx=int(np.argmax(c)); w[idx]=1
   else: w/=s
   return w
```
If c has many zeros, M-mean small, t small; exp may underflow many. Fine.

Log update:
```
def _log_update(a, d, eta, rng, noise):
   loga=np.log(a+1e-300)
   mu=np.dot(a,d)
   dev=d-mu
   var=np.dot(a,dev*dev)
   if var<1e-300: return a
   loga -= eta*dev/np.sqrt(var)
   if noise>1e-8:
      loga += rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
   loga -= loga.max()
   # To avoid extremely small and large, clip loga to [-80,0]
   loga=np.clip(loga, -80, 0)
   an=enp.exp(loga)
   an = an[an>1e-300] # no, keep length
   an[an<1e-300]=0
   s=an.sum()
   if s<=0 or not finite: return a
   an/=s
   return an
```
Clipping to -80 means min ~1e-35, okay. This prevents underflow and keeps positive. But zeros allowed; small positives can slightly affect score but negligible. It also prevents the optimizer from fully eliminating bad entries; may be okay. If exact optimum zeros, small floor reduces score tiny.

Use noise: start 0.05, decay 0.99; add to loga before normalization. This helps escape. Could be too much; adapt.

Parameter adapt:
- `eta` start 0.5, max 1.2, min 0.05.
- `frac` temperature start 0.05 (top 5% range), maybe increase to 0.2 over time to consider more peaks? Actually to flatten, focus on highest; if no improvement, broaden. We'll do if rejected: frac*=0.7 (cooler? smaller t), if accepted many no improvement: frac*=1.2. Simpler: keep 0.1.
- `noise` start 0.1 for random around antipal, 0.05 for smooth.

Maybe the optimizer can get stuck if loga clipped and no variance. Noise breaks.

```
   a[np.isnan(a)] = 0 ...
```

```
def resample(parent, n):
   if parent.size==1: return np.ones(n)
   return np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,parent.size), parent)
```

Search function:
```
def run_search(n, starts, max_time, exact_max, eta0=0.6):
   L=1 << (3*n-1).bit_length()
   def square(a):
      A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
      c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:2*n-1]
      c=0.5*(c+c[::-1]); c=np.clip(c,0,None); return c,A
   def grad(a, w, A):
      W=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], L)
      h=np.fft.irfft(A*W, L)
      d=h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
      return np.clip(d,0,None)
   def update(a, d, eta, frac, temp? noise):
       ...
   for a0 in starts:
       if elapsed>max_time: break
       a=a0.copy()
       # exact initial if time
       try_exact(a, force=False)
       eta=eta0; frac=0.08; noise=0.08
       reject_streak=0
       for it in range(20000):
          if elapsed>max_time: break
          c,A=square(a)
          M=float(c.max())
          score=2.0*n*M
          # exact if promising
          if score < best['score'] + 1e-12:
              if exact_allowed: try_exact(a, score)
          # update
          mean=float(c.mean())
          scale=max(M-mean,1e-15)
          t=max(frac*scale, 1e-15)
          w=np.exp((c-M)/t)
          sw=w.sum()
          if not finite or sw==0: top weights
          else w/=sw
          d=grad(a,w,A)
          mu=float(np.dot(a,d))
          dev=d-mu
          var=float(np.dot(a,dev*dev))
          if var <= 1e-25:
              # try noise restart
              if noise > 1e-6:
                  a = normalize(a + rng.normal(0,0.02/a.size? maybe))
                  continue
              break
          an=update(...)
          c2,A2=square(an)
          M2=c2.max()
          if M2 <= M * (1+1e-14) or it % 50 == 0:
              accept
              a=an; c=c2; A=A2; M=M2
              reject_streak=0
              # eta adapt small
              if M2 < score/(2*n)*(1-1e-8): eta=min(1.5,eta*1.03)
              else: eta=max(0.1,eta*0.95)
          else:
              reject_streak +=1
              # try half step
              an_half=update(a, d, eta*0.5, ...)
              c3=square(an_half); M3=...
              if M3 <= M*(1+1e-14):
                 a=an_half; accept; eta*=0.8
              else:
                 eta*=0.7
                 if reject_streak>10:
                     # broaden temperature and noise, maybe break
                     frac = min(0.5, frac*1.3)
                     noise = min(0.5, noise*1.5)
                     if noise < 1e-8 or reject_streak>50:
                         break
          # exact if accepted and score improves? handled next loop; can exact now if M2 < best - margin
          if M2 < best['score']/(2*n) - 1e-8 and exact_allowed:
              try_exact(an)
          if best['score'] < 1.50 and elapsed < time... maybe continue
          noise *= 0.995
          frac *= 1.005
```

```
import numpy as np
import time, math

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try: budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    except: budget=1300.0
    if budget <= 0: return [1.0]
    # n
    n = 4096
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size > 0 and np.isfinite(p).any():
            n = int(p.size)
    except Exception:
        pass
    if budget < 30: n = min(n, 256)
    elif budget < 120: n = min(n, 1024)
    n = int(min(max(n, 1), 8192))
    global_deadline = start + max(1.0, budget - 10.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best = {'score': 2.0, 'arr': np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)}
    surr_best_score = float('inf'); surr_best = None
    exact = {'count':0, 'last':start, 'max':0, 'gap':1.0}
    target = 1.50274349
```

Try_exact:
```
    def try_exact(a, hint=None, force=False):
        if time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 2.0: return
        if not force:
            if exact['count'] >= exact['max']: return
            if time.monotonic() - exact['last'] < exact['gap']: return
            if hint is not None and hint > best['score'] + 0.05: return
        try:
            sc = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            return
        exact['count'] += 1; exact['last']=time.monotonic()
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best['score'] - 1e-12:
            best['score'] = sc
            best['arr'] = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
```

Normalize:
```
    def normalize(x):
        a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0: return None
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
            a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
            s=1.0
        a /= s
        return a
```
Clipping to 1000 before normalization might be wrong if all values >1000? But init not huge. If a contains huge, clip then normalize; fine.

Parent resample:
```
    def get_parent(n out):
       try:
          p=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          p=np.clip(p,0,1000); p=np.where(np.isfinite(p),p,0)
          if p.size==0 or p.sum()<=1e-15: return None
          if p.size==out: return p/p.sum()
          x=np.linspace(0,1,p.size); y=np.linspace(0,1,out)
          r=np.interp(y,x,p); return r/r.sum()
       except: return None
```
Starts:
```
    def make_starts(n, max_starts, include_array=None):
       st=[]
       def push(a):
          aa=normalize(a)
          if aa is not None and aa.size==n and np.all(np.isfinite(aa)) and aa.sum()>0:
             st.append(aa)
       if include_array is not None:
          push(np.interp(np.linspace(0,1,n), np.linspace(0,1,include_array.size), include_array))
       # anti-pal
       for alpha in (0.85, 0.97):
          x=rng.normal(0,1,n)
          idx=np.arange(n); j=n-1-idx
          mask=idx<j
          x[j[mask]]=-x[idx[mask]]
          if n%2==1: x[n//2]=0.0
          x-=x.mean(); sd=x.std(); if sd>0: x/=sd
          push(0.5 + alpha*x)
       # sine
       ii=np.arange(n, dtype=float)/n
       push(1.0 + 0.9*np.sin(2*np.pi*ii) + 0.4*np.sin(4*np.pi*ii))
       push(1.0 + 0.8*np.sin(6*np.pi*ii) + 0.5*np.sin(2*np.pi*ii))
       # random positive
       push(1.0 + rng.random(n))
       push(1.0 + 3.0*rng.exponential(1.0, n))
       # exp ramp
       push(np.exp(3.0*np.linspace(0,1,n)))
       push(np.exp(-3.0*np.linspace(0,1,n)))
       # binary random
       for p in (0.4,0.5,0.6):
          b=(rng.random(n)<p).astype(float); b += 1.0/n
          push(b)
       # parent
       par=... ; if par is not None: push(par)
       # power
       push(np.power(np.arange(1,n+1, dtype=float), -0.3))
       push(np.power(np.arange(n,0,-1, dtype=float), -0.3))
       # random smooth: kernel? maybe not.
       # deduplicate? not necessary, limit
       if len(st)>max_starts: st=st[:max_starts]
       return st
```
Order: start with include_array (coarse best), anti-pal, sine, random, parent. Good.

```
    def run_search(n, starts, max_time, exact_max, gap, eta0=0.6):
        ex = {'count':0, 'last':time.monotonic(), 'max':exact_max, 'gap':gap}
        def try_ex(a, hint=None, force=False):
            if time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 3.0: return
            if not force:
                if ex['count'] >= ex['max']: return
                if time.monotonic() - ex['last'] < ex['gap']: return
                if hint is not None and hint > best['score'] + 0.05: return
            try: sc=float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
            except Exception: return
            ex['count']+=1; ex['last']=time.monotonic()
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best['score'] - 1e-12:
                best['score']=sc; best['arr']=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy()
        L=1 << (3*n -1).bit_length()
        clen=2*n-1
        def square(a):
           A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
           c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:clen]
           c=0.5*(c+c[::-1])
           c=np.clip(c,0.0,None)
           return c,A
        def grad(a,w,A):
           W=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],L)
           h=np.fft.irfft(A*W,L)
           d=h[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
           d=np.clip(d,0.0,None)
           return d
        def do_update(a,d,eta,noise):
           loga=np.log(a+1e-300)
           mu=float(np.dot(a,d))
           dev=d-mu
           var=float(np.dot(a,dev*dev))
           if not np.isfinite(var) or var <= 1e-30:
               return None
           std=math.sqrt(var)
           loga -= (eta*dev)/std
           if noise > 1e-8:
               loga += rng.normal(0.0, noise, n)
           loga -= loga.max()
           if not np.all(np.isfinite(loga)):
               loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -50.0)
           loga = np.clip(loga, -50.0, 0.0)
           an=np.exp(loga)
           s=an.sum()
           if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0: return None
           an /= s
           return an
        search_end = min(global_deadline, time.monotonic() + max(max_time,1.0))
        local_surr_score = float('inf'); local_surr_arr=None
        for a0 in starts:
            a = np.array(a0, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
            # initial exact
            try_ex(a, hint=2.0*n*np.max(np.clip(???)), force=False) # can't compute c; maybe simple.
            # initial exact if cheap: compute c once? we'll do in loop.
            eta=eta0; frac=0.08; noise=0.08
            reject=0
            it=0
            while time.monotonic() < search_end:
                it += 1
                c,A=square(a)
                M=float(c.max())
                score=2.0*n*M
                if score < local_surr_score - 1e-10 * max(1.0,local_surr_score):
                    local_surr_score = score; local_surr_arr = a.copy()
                # exact
                if score < best['score'] - 1e-7:
                    try_ex(a, hint=score)
                elif score < best['score'] + 0.3 and it % 50 == 0:
                    try_ex(a, hint=score)
                scale = max(M - float(c.mean()), 1e-15)
                t = max(frac*scale, 1e-15)
                w = np.exp((c - M)/t)
                sw = w.sum()
                if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0:
                    w = np.zeros(clen); w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
                else:
                    w /= sw
                d = grad(a,w,A)
                an = do_update(a,d,eta,noise)
                if an is None:
                    # try noise restart
                    if noise > 1e-6:
                        a = normalize(a + 0.1*rng.normal(0,1,n))
                        if a is None: break
                    else:
                        break
                an2 = an
                c2,A2 = square(an2)
                M2 = float(c2.max())
                if M2 <= M * (1.0 + 1e-12):
                    a=an2; c=c2; A=A2; M=M2; reject=0
                    if M2 < M * (1.0 - 1e-8):
                        eta = min(1.5, eta*1.02)
                    else:
                        eta = max(0.1, eta*0.98)
                else:
                    # half step
                    an_half = do_update(a,d,eta*0.5,noise)
                    if an_half is not None:
                        cH= square(an_half); MH=float(cH.max())
                        if MH <= M*(1.0+1e-12):
                            a=an_half; c=cH; _=square? Need A2 for c? We can recompute? set M=MH; M2=MH; reject=0; eta*=0.85
                        else:

                            eta *= 0.7

                            if reject > 20:

                                if a is None: break
                                reject=0
```

Let's review mathematical correctness of gradient update one more time. `c = a * a` (index sum). Derivative of c_k wrt a_i is a_{k-i} if in range. The gradient of smooth max with weights `w_k` is `d_i = sum_k w_k a_{k-i}`. Our grad computes `d_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}`. Since k=i+j. Yes. It uses wr reversed. For i, d_i=h[2n-2-i] where h=a*wr. We slice h[n-1:2n-1][::-1]. Let's test n=2: a=[a0,a1], w length3 [w0,w1,w2]. d0=a0w0+a1w1. d1=a0w1+a1w2. wr=[w2,w1,w0]. h length4: h0=a0w2; h1=a0w1+a1w2; h2=a0w0+a1w1; h3=a1w0. slice h[n-1:2n-1]=h[1:3]=[h1,h2], reversed=[h2,h1]=d0,d1. good.

Square FFT: For a normalized sum1, c length 2n-1. Symmetrization: Since convolution of real with itself, c symmetric around n-1. `c[::-1]` length same. Average good. If small negative, clip.

`np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)` with A complex squared may produce small imaginary not used.

If L not large enough for gradient: L = next pow 3n-1, h length before truncation n + (2n-1)-1 =3n-2 ≤ L? For n=1, 2? 3n-1=2 next pow 2? 1<< (2).bit_length() -> 4, okay. For n=6733, 3n-1=20198, bit_length15, 1<<15=32768 ≥20197. Good.

```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    """
    Minimize the exact evaluator score.

    The evaluator computes
        score = 2*n*max_k (a*a)[k] / (sum a)^2
    where a is clipped to [0, 1000].  The problem is scale-invariant; the
    routine normalizes internally to sum(a)=1 and searches for nonnegative
    shapes with a flatter square-convolution profile.

    The search combines:
      * deterministic and stochastic nonnegative initializations,
      * an anti-palindromic/low-center initialization that attacks the central
        convolution peak,
      * smooth-max gradient updates in log-space, implemented matrix-free via
        FFT convolutions,
      * exact-evaluation checkpoints with the authoritative evaluator.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 1300.0
    if budget <= 1.0:
        return [1.0]

    target = 1.50274349
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Choose a working length.  The parent length is used when reasonable.
    n = 4096
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if p.size > 0 and np.isfinite(p).any():
            n = int(p.size)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if budget < 30.0:
        n = min(n, 256)
    elif budget < 120.0:
        n = min(n, 1024)

    n = int(min(max(n, 1), 8192))
    global_deadline = t_start + max(1.0, budget - 10.0)

    # Authoritative best so far.  [1.0] has exact score 2 and is always valid.
    best = {"score": 2.0, "arr": np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)}

    def _normalize(x):
        a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return None
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
            a = np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
            s = 1.0
        a /= s
        return a

    def _parent_resampled(out_n):
        try:
            p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if p.size == 0:
                return None
            p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(p.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
                return None
            if p.size == out_n:
                p /= s
                return p
            if p.size == 1:
                return np.full(out_n, float(p[0]) / s + 1e-300)
            x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
            y = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, out_n)
            r = np.interp(y, x, p)
            return r

    def _resample_array(arr, out_n):
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return None
        if arr.size == 1:
            return np.full(out_n, float(arr[0]))
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, arr.size)
        y = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, out_n)
        return np.interp(y, x, arr)

    def _make_starts(out_n, max_starts, include_array=None):
        starts = []

        def push(a):
            aa = _normalize(a)
            if aa is not None and aa.size == out_n and np.all(np.isfinite(aa)) and float(aa.sum()) > 0.0:
                starts.append(aa)

        # Interpolate a previously found good shape, if any.
        if include_array is not None:
            ra = _resample_array(include_array, out_n)
            if ra is not None:
                push(ra)

        ii = np.arange(out_n, dtype=np.float64)
        xx = ii / max(1, out_n - 1) if out_n > 1 else np.zeros(out_n)

        # Anti-palindromic signed patterns: reduce the central coefficient
        # while leaving many degrees of freedom for the optimizer.
        for alpha in (0.85, 0.97):
            x = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, out_n)
            idx = np.arange(out_n)
            j = out_n - 1 - idx
            mask = idx < j
            x[j[mask]] = -x[idx[mask]]
            if out_n % 2 == 1:
                x[out_n // 2] = 0.0
            x -= x.mean()
            sd = x.std()
            if sd > 1e-15:
                x /= sd
            push(0.5 + alpha * x)

        # Deterministic anti-symmetric sine mixtures.
        push(1.0 + 0.9 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * xx) + 0.4 * np.sin(4.0 * np.pi * xx))
        push(1.0 + 0.8 * np.sin(6.0 * np.pi * xx) + 0.5 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * xx))
        push(1.0 + 0.7 * np.sin(10.0 * np.pi * xx) + 0.4 * np.sin(4.0 * np.pi * xx))

        # Smooth random envelopes.
        push(1.0 + rng.random(out_n))
        push(1.0 + 3.0 * rng.exponential(1.0, out_n))
        w = np.cumsum(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, out_n))
        w = w - w.min()
        push(0.5 + w / (w.max() + 1e-15))

        # One-sided ramps.
        push(np.exp(3.0 * xx))
        push(np.exp(-3.0 * xx))
        push((ii + 1.0) ** (-0.25))
        push((out_n - ii) ** (-0.25))

        # Balanced binary-ish starts.
        for pb in (0.4, 0.5, 0.6):
            b = (rng.random(out_n) < pb).astype(np.float64)
            b += 1.0 / out_n
            push(b)

        # Parent construction, resampled if necessary.
        par = _parent_resampled(out_n)
        if par is not None:
            push(par)

        if max_starts > 0 and len(starts) > max_starts:
            starts = starts[:max_starts]
        return starts

    def _run_search(out_n, starts, max_time, exact_max, exact_gap, eta0=0.6):
        if out_n <= 0 or not starts:
            return

        L = 1 << (3 * out_n - 1).bit_length()
        clen = 2 * out_n - 1

        ex = {"count": 0, "last": time.monotonic(), "max": int(max(exact_max, 0)), "gap": float(exact_gap)}

        def try_exact(a, hint=None, force=False):
            if time.monotonic() > global_deadline - 3.0:
                return
            if not force:
                if ex["count"] >= ex["max"]:
                    return
                if time.monotonic() - ex["last"] < ex["gap"]:
                    return
                if hint is not None and hint > best["score"] + 0.05:
                    return
            try:
                sc = float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
            except Exception:
                return
            ex["count"] += 1
            ex["last"] = time.monotonic()
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best["score"] - 1e-12:
                best["score"] = sc
                best["arr"] = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()

        def square_fft(a):
            A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
            c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:clen]
            c = 0.5 * (c + c[::-1])
            c = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)
            return c, A

        def grad_from_weights(a, w, A):
            # d_i = sum_k w_k a_{k-i}, computed by one linear convolution.
            W = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], L)
            h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, L)
            d = h[out_n - 1:2 * out_n - 1][::-1]
            return np.clip(d, 0.0, None)

        def log_update(a, d, eta, noise):
            loga = np.log(a + 1e-300)
            mu = float(np.dot(a, d))
            dev = d - mu
            var = float(np.dot(a, dev * dev))
            if not np.isfinite(var) or var <= 1e-30:
                return None
            std = math.sqrt(var)
            loga -= (eta * dev) / std
            if noise > 1e-8:
                loga += rng.normal(0.0, noise, out_n)
            loga -= loga.max()
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(loga)):
                loga = np.where(np.isfinite(loga), loga, -50.0)
            loga = np.clip(loga, -50.0, 0.0)
            an = np.exp(loga)
            s = float(an.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
                return None
            an /= s
            return an

        search_end = min(global_deadline, time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(max_time)))
        local_surr_score = float("inf")
        local_surr_arr = None

        for a0 in starts:
            a = np.array(a0, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)
            c, A = square_fft(a)
            M = float(c.max())
            score = 2.0 * out_n * M

            if score < local_surr_score:
                local_surr_score = score
                local_surr_arr = a.copy()
            try_exact(a, hint=score)

            eta = float(eta0)
            frac = 0.08
            noise = 0.08
            reject = 0
            it = 0

            while time.monotonic() < search_end:
                it += 1

                mean_c = float(c.mean())
                scale = max(M - mean_c, 1e-15)
                temp = max(frac * scale, 1e-15)

                w = np.exp((c - M) / temp)
                sw = float(w.sum())
                if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
                    w = np.zeros(clen, dtype=np.float64)
                    w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
                else:
                    w /= sw

                d = grad_from_weights(a, w, A)
                an = log_update(a, d, eta, noise)

                if an is None:
                    if noise < 0.5:
                        noise = max(0.2, noise * 2.5)
                        a = _normalize(a + 0.15 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, out_n))
                        if a is None:
                            break
                        c, A = square_fft(a)
                        M = float(c.max())
                        continue
                    break

                c2, A2 = square_fft(an)
                M2 = float(c2.max())
                accepted = False

                if M2 <= M * (1.0 + 1e-12):
                    a = an
                    c = c2
                    A = A2
                    M = M2
                    accepted = True
                    if M2 < M * (1.0 - 1e-8):
                        eta = min(1.5, eta * 1.02)
                    else:
                        eta = max(0.08, eta * 0.98)
                else:
                    an_half = log_update(a, d, eta * 0.5, noise)
                    if an_half is not None:
                        cH, AH = square_fft(an_half)
                        MH = float(cH.max())
                        if MH <= M * (1.0 + 1e-12):
                            a = an_half
                            c = cH
                            A = AH
                            M = MH
                            accepted = True
                            eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.85)
                        else:
                            reject += 1
                            eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.7)
                            frac = min(0.5, frac * 1.25)
                            noise = min(0.8, noise * 1.3)
                            if reject > 25:
                                a = _normalize(a + 0.25 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, out_n))
                                if a is None:
                                    break
                                c, A = square_fft(a)
                                M = float(c.max())
                                reject = 0
                            if reject > 80:
                                break

                score = 2.0 * out_n * M
                if score < local_surr_score:
                    local_surr_score = score
                    local_surr_arr = a.copy()

                if score < best["score"] - 1e-7:
                    try_exact(a, hint=score)
                elif score < best["score"] + 0.3 and it % 70 == 0:
                    try_exact(a, hint=score)

                if accepted:
                    if noise > 1e-9:
                        noise *= 0.998
                    frac = min(0.5, frac * 1.002)
                else:
                    if noise > 1e-9:
                        noise *= 0.999

                if best["score"] < target and it > 800 and time.monotonic() > search_end - 5.0:
                    break

        if (
            local_surr_arr is not None
            and local_surr_score < best["score"] - 1e-9
            and time.monotonic() < global_deadline - 3.0
        ):
            try_exact(local_surr_arr, force=True)

    # Phase 1: a short coarse search.  If it finds a usable shape, it is
    # interpolated as an initialization for the full-length search.
    coarse_time = 0.0
    include_for_full = None

    if n > 1024 and budget > 120.0:
        nc = min(1024, n)
        starts_c = _make_starts(nc, 6, include_array=None)
        ct = min(60.0, 0.15 * budget)
        _run_search(nc, starts_c, ct, exact_max=400, exact_gap=0.1)
        coarse_time = time.monotonic() - t_start
        if best["arr"].size == nc:
            include_for_full = best["arr"]

    # Phase 2: full / target length search.
    nl = n
    starts_l = _make_starts(nl, 10, include_array=include_for_full)
    rem = max(20.0, budget - coarse_time - 25.0)
    if nl > 4000:
        _run_search(nl, starts_l, rem, exact_max=80, exact_gap=2.0)
    else:
        _run_search(nl, starts_l, rem, exact_max=300, exact_gap=0.3)

    # Final validation / safe fallback.
    final_arr = best["arr"]
    if final_arr is None or np.ndim(final_arr) != 1 or final_arr.size == 0:
        final_arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    final_arr = np.asarray(final_arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    final_arr = np.where(np.isfinite(final_arr), final_arr, 0.0)
    final_arr = np.clip(final_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    # A truly tiny array is acceptable, but guarantee a finite sum.
    if float(final_arr.sum()) < 0.01:
        final_arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    # If we still have headroom, revalidate the chosen candidate with the
    # authoritative evaluator.  If anything is wrong, fall back to [1.0].
    if time.monotonic() < global_deadline - 2.0 and final_arr.size <= 8192:
        try:
            final_sc = float(evaluate_sequence(final_arr.tolist()))
            if not np.isfinite(final_sc):
                final_arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        except Exception:
            final_arr = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    return [float(x) for x in final_arr]
```
